# ProofPay AI implementation progress

Last updated: 2026-11-06 (Africa/Lagos)

Status values: **Not started**, **In progress**, **Implemented**, **Tested**, **Blocked**. “Implemented” means code exists; “Tested” means the recorded verification was actually run. A blocked external credential does not block independent implementation.

## Environment and baseline

| Item | Status | Evidence / notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Native Windows environment | Tested | PowerShell reports `Windows_NT`; this is not WSL. |
| Node.js | Tested | `v26.13.0`; installed `C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe` is present. |
| npm | Tested | `v11.20.0` from `C:\Program Files\nodejs\npm.cmd`. `C:\Program Files\nodejs` was added to User PATH. Refreshed process PATH resolves `node.exe`, `npm.cmd`, and `npx.cmd`; restart Codex if the running app process has not inherited the updated User PATH. |
| Git | Available | Git `2.53.0.windows.3`; repository exists on `master`. Git metadata writes are not available in the current sandbox. |
| APIMatic PayPal TypeScript skills | Available | `typescript-integrate-paypal` plus companion skills found under the configured skills directory. |
| APIMatic PayPal Context Plugin | Tested | Installed into Codex and VS Code on 2026-10-29. In this refreshed session the PayPal TypeScript integration, getting-started, error-handling, and testing workflows were opened before implementation. Invoicing uses direct REST because the inspected SDK map has no invoice resource. See `docs/APIMATIC_INTEGRATION.md`. |
| APIMatic-generated PayPal SDK map | Tested | Inspected the package map: it lists Orders, Payments, Vault, Transaction Search, and Subscriptions, but not Invoicing. The temporary extraneous package was removed after inspection; no generated SDK runtime dependency remains. The discovery-only child lock is ignored; the workspace root lock is authoritative. |
| PayPal Sandbox credentials | Tested | Sandbox client ID/secret are configured and successfully authenticated against the fixed Sandbox OAuth endpoint. PAYPAL_WEBHOOK_ID remains a placeholder. No credential values were displayed. |
| Supabase credentials | Tested | Private root `.env` is configured. The linked ProofPayAI Staging project, Auth, database connection, migrations, and authenticated API sessions were verified without printing credential values. |
| AI provider credentials | Tested | Groq is configured server-side. Real Groq milestone generation and one scope analysis with persisted audit passed; OpenAI remains an untested alternative. No key values were displayed. |
| Docker / Postman | Not available by command name | The official Supabase CLI is installed as a workspace development dependency and runs through `npx.cmd`; no local PostgreSQL client/server or Docker runtime is available for engine-level migration testing. Postman collections are present, but no Postman CLI/runtime is configured. |
| Workspace typecheck, tests, and build | Tested | Root workspace `typecheck`, `test`, and `build` scripts passed after the foundation and frontend preview were added. See phase check records below. |

## Phase status

| Phase | Status | Verification / outstanding work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 — Foundation | Tested | Native Windows toolchain, workspace/docs/env template, API health foundation, shared package, migration layout, PayPal contract plan, and design tokens established. Workspace typecheck, tests, and build passed. |
| 1 — Premium frontend | Tested | Responsive landing page, auth/recovery preview, role-aware freelancer/client workspace shell, dashboard, projects, agreement/milestones, evidence-backed sample scope review, and honest PayPal-not-connected invoice state are implemented. UI interactions, production build, and workspace suite passed; provider workflows remain previews. |
| 2 — Core project functionality | In progress | All 18 approved migrations are recorded on confirmed ProofPayAI Staging. Real client/freelancer sessions verified fixed-price project state, hourly agreement and time-entry workflows, and unrelated-account isolation. No local PostgreSQL/Docker runtime is available. |
| 3 — AI intelligence | In progress | Groq milestone generation and evidence-backed scope analysis have both passed with real provider responses; the scope audit persisted under the authenticated actor. OpenAI real-provider behavior, structured agreement drafting, and saved agreement approval remain outstanding. |
| 4 — PayPal/APIMatic | In progress | Direct REST Invoicing API v2 remains fixed to the Sandbox host; credentials are backend-only. One real Sandbox OAuth request returned HTTP 200 with Bearer token type, positive expiration, and recognized Invoicing access. One fictional USD 600 invoice was created once, verified as DRAFT, and sent once; provider and local state are SENT. Migration 18 is applied and its database queue ACLs, RLS, indexes, functions, and regression trigger were verified. Payment and live webhook delivery remain unverified; the webhook ID is a placeholder and there is no public HTTPS callback. |
| 5 — Sponsor features | Tested | Render Blueprint, secret-free Postman collection, role-scoped AG Grid Community analytics, and a responsive CSS milestone timeline are implemented and workspace-checked. On 2026-11-03, the Render CLI accepted the Free-plan Staging Blueprint against the verified workspace and listed exactly `proofpay-staging-api` and `proofpay-staging-web`; this was validation only, with no sync or deployment. Bryntum is not installed because no project license/package is configured; the existing timeline covers the workflow without adding an unlicensed dependency. The AG Grid chunk-size warning remains visible. |
| 6 — Quality assurance | In progress | After applying migration 18, `npm.cmd run typecheck`, `npm.cmd test` (275 tests: 24 shared, 117 API, 15 web, 119 scripts/SQL contracts), focused webhook tests (7 SQL-contract plus 38 API/worker tests), and `npm.cmd run build` passed. The existing AG Grid chunk warning remains at 719.63 kB minified / 198.47 kB gzip. No lint script, automated accessibility runner, or local PostgreSQL/Docker runtime is configured. |
| 7 — Hackathon readiness | In progress | Root/workspace setup docs, a preview-honest demo script, Devpost draft, and submission checklist are drafted. Local startup commands and internal Markdown links were verified. Free Render configuration and its cold-start/webhook limitations are documented; the read-only Blueprint check passed, but no public deployment or recording exists. |
| 8 — Marketplace identity and profiles | In progress | Protected role selection, private client profiles, freelancer editing/portfolio, and the opt-in public projection are implemented. On staging, the freelancer profile save and private read passed under the freelancer's authenticated API session; anonymous public lookup returned 404 while `is_public=false`. Reviews, work history, and full account lifecycle remain outstanding. |
| 9 — Jobs, proposals, and hiring | In progress | Fixed/hourly job, proposal, offer, and hiring records are persisted in staging. The new `202611010002` migration fixes the ambiguous offer replay hash. Real staging verified one offer creation, identical retry returning the same ID with one stored row, changed details returning HTTP 409, and unrelated-account access/reuse/accept attempts being denied without changing the offer. Client/freelancer reads of existing fixed and hourly projects still return 200; unrelated reads return 404. Broader proposal/moderation RLS and AI/provider workflows remain. |
| 10 — Messaging and notifications | In progress | Staging API tests passed for two participant messages, notification creation, single-notification read, conversation read receipts, and unread-count retrieval. The unrelated authenticated user received no private rows before these writes, and unauthorized message send was denied. The post-write repeat of cross-account checks was not reached after the later hourly 403. Storage, Realtime, older-history pagination, non-project marketplace DMs, and malware scanning remain unverified/outstanding. |
| 11 — Hourly project workflows | In progress | All 18 migrations are recorded. Both client/freelancer sessions read the active contract. A manual time entry was created and idempotently replayed, submitted, disputed by the client, edited/resubmitted by the freelancer, and approved by the client. The approved 4,500-second entry persisted and appeared as an exact-amount invoice candidate. The unrelated user saw no private rows and could not review, update, or delete the entry. Hourly invoice creation/send and timer workflows remain untested; scope is frozen unless needed for a demo regression. |
| 12 — Commercial hardening | In progress | All 18 migrations are recorded on ProofPayAI Staging. The latest catalog check confirms offer creation is executable by authenticated and not anon, `marketplace_offers` RLS remains enabled with its participant-only policy, and the user-scoped view uses invoker rights. Admin moderation and account deletion workflows, backups/restore, monitoring, account-lifecycle policy, and authorized Render deployment remain outstanding; marketplace expansion is frozen during demo completion. |
| 13 — Real AI and PayPal Sandbox verification | In progress | Real Groq milestone generation and scope analysis passed; the latter persisted a Zod- and citation-validated audit. One real PayPal Sandbox OAuth call passed HTTP, token-type, positive-expiration, and recognized Invoicing-scope checks. One USD 600 Sandbox invoice was created once, verified as DRAFT, and sent once; provider and local state are SENT. Payment and live webhook tests remain unverified because the webhook ID is a placeholder and no public HTTPS callback is configured. The unrelated-user access test for the scope audit remains unverified. |

- Captured the inspected release branch baseline at **84f588fbe918b718649e888665a387577db62d43**, preserved pre-existing working-tree changes, and recorded the latest migration 19 failure without claiming an unidentified root cause.
- This documentation pass did not run application tests, typecheck, build, lint, database commands, migrations, PayPal/AI calls, or deployments. No application logic, schema, or test behavior was changed.

### Historical blockers recorded after migration 202610310003 (superseded by later verification entries)

- Supabase Staging is configured and verified. All ten existing migrations are recorded remotely. Reproduction identified the exact hourly `42501`: the initially-deferred `assert_accepted_agreement_total` trigger runs as the authenticated role and its SELECT on `public.hourly_contracts` is denied because that role has no table SELECT grant. The API's SECURITY DEFINER RPC and role/party checks are intact. Proposed migration 202610310004 changes only the invariant trigger's execution context/path and revokes direct EXECUTE on the two trigger-only functions. It is not applied; obtain separate approval before database changes.
- Confirmed staging workflows: three existing fictional accounts authenticated; freelancer private-profile save/read persisted; fixed-price dual acceptance and milestone start/submission/approval passed; participant messaging, notifications, unread count, and read receipts passed. Initial unrelated-user reads returned no private rows, and unauthorized acceptance/start/message operations were denied. Repeat cross-account reads after the newly added records; that post-write pass was not reached.
- Catalog checks found RLS on all 44 public tables and 43 policies; private views use invoker/barrier settings and deny anonymous SELECT, while the two intended freelancer projections remain public. `is_active_hourly_project` remains service-role-only. Both `assert_accepted_agreement_total` and `notify_project_message` have inherited PUBLIC/anon/authenticated/service_role EXECUTE ACLs; anonymous PostgREST RPC attempts were rejected because they are trigger-return functions. The prepared migration revokes those direct calls while preserving installed triggers.
- No local PostgreSQL server, `psql`, or Docker runtime is available. Migration execution and database catalog/function behavior were verified on staging, but there was no separate local PostgreSQL engine run.
- Live PayPal Sandbox invoice/webhook testing remains blocked on privately configured Sandbox client ID/secret, webhook ID, and a public HTTPS callback. No invoice, payment, or webhook operation was performed. The official PayPal Invoicing API v2 REST integration and APIMatic evidence remain intact.
- Live AI testing remains blocked on an authorized provider key/model. No provider call occurred; the structured-output adapter and mock tests remain in place.
- Staging administrator moderation and account-deletion request/cancellation workflows have not been exercised with a test administrator/owner. Backup/restore evidence, monitoring/alerting, and account-retention/deactivation policy remain outstanding.
- Render deployment was not performed and still requires separate authorization. The production build passes locally; hosted Blueprint configuration and health checks remain unverified.
- No workspace lint script or automated accessibility runner is configured. Keyboard-only, contrast, screen-reader, performance, and complete accessibility coverage remain outstanding. AG Grid still emits a 719.63 kB minified / 198.47 kB gzip chunk warning.
- The sample dashboard remains intentionally demo data. Registration and recovery were not exercised because staging email confirmation is enabled and no controlled inbox was authorized. The existing fictional fixtures and created record IDs are listed in `docs/STAGING_TEST_FIXTURES.md` for later cleanup with separate authorization.

## Phase 13 — real AI and PayPal Sandbox integration verification

### Read-only audit and configuration preflight

- Secret-safe local `.env` presence check: `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `OPENAI_MODEL` are not configured; `PAYPAL_SANDBOX_CLIENT_ID`, `PAYPAL_SANDBOX_CLIENT_SECRET`, and `PAYPAL_WEBHOOK_ID` are not configured. The check printed only booleans and never emitted values. Public frontend Supabase URL/key are present; `.env` is ignored by Git. No PayPal/OpenAI/service-role secret is referenced by `apps/web` source or a `VITE_*` variable.
- AI code review: `apps/api/src/ai.ts` sends OpenAI Responses requests from Node only, uses `store:false`, JSON Schema structured output, a 25-second abort, and a bounded output size. `/api/ai/milestones` validates the request and generated milestones with shared Zod schemas, computes exact minor-unit allocations server-side, and returns a human-review draft without persistence. Scope analysis reads the authenticated user's RLS-visible request/current accepted agreement evidence, validates the schema and exact citations, and only then records the result through the service-role-only audit RPC. Routes are rate limited and do not approve changes or payment. No distinct AI contract-analysis endpoint exists in the current source.
- PayPal code review: server configuration accepts only `PAYPAL_SANDBOX_*` client credentials plus a webhook ID; the client host is fixed to `https://api-m.sandbox.paypal.com`. OAuth and all Invoicing API v2 calls remain in the Node adapter. Invoice creation is preceded by a database claim that validates an approved milestone or an eligible hourly batch; sending has its own claim. Unknown write outcomes are reconciled by invoice number or invoice detail, and are not retried automatically. Webhook events are processed only after PayPal signature verification, then the current invoice is fetched from PayPal; the database deduplicates by unique provider event ID. Client/freelancer API routes do not mark invoices paid.
- APIMatic evidence remains in `docs/APIMATIC_INTEGRATION.md` and the contract remains in `paypal-plan.md`. The inspected SDK map has no Invoicing resource, so direct REST is still the documented choice; no unrelated SDK operation was substituted or removed.
- No AI or PayPal provider request, Supabase request, database write, migration, invoice, payment, webhook registration, deployment, or staging-data modification was performed during this preflight. Thirteen migrations remain the last previously verified staging history.

### Provider verification blockers and approval plan

- Real AI generation is blocked until the account owner configures Groq or OpenAI variables in the ignored root `.env`. First live test should be one fictional milestone-generation request; it returns an editable draft and does not write application records. A live scope-analysis test writes an `ai_analysis_records` audit row and requires separate approval to create/use fictional staging request data. Groq scope persistence has an additional database compatibility blocker described below.
- PayPal verification is blocked until the owner configures a Sandbox app's client ID and secret plus its registered webhook ID in the ignored root `.env`, and provides a reachable HTTPS webhook endpoint. Configure only `PAYPAL_SANDBOX_CLIENT_ID`, `PAYPAL_SANDBOX_CLIENT_SECRET`, and `PAYPAL_WEBHOOK_ID`; do not send values in chat. The first provider check should be a separately approved Sandbox OAuth call only. Draft invoice creation, invoice sending to a Sandbox test recipient, and a Sandbox payer payment/webhook exercise each need their own explicit approval. Use only authorized fictional Sandbox accounts; no real recipient or money.
- Phase 13 is **not complete** and ProofPay AI is **not yet ready for a real-provider controlled demo deployment**. Remaining operational blockers include real AI/PayPal/webhook verification, monitoring/alerting, backup/restore rehearsal, and outstanding account lifecycle/moderation work recorded under Phases 8 and 12. No Render deployment was attempted.

### GroqCloud provider implementation (2026-11-01)

- Added a backend-only Groq Chat Completions adapter while preserving the existing OpenAI Responses adapter. `AI_PROVIDER` selects either provider; Groq is the default/preferred selection, and an OpenAI-only legacy environment remains compatible when `AI_PROVIDER` is unset. Groq defaults to `openai/gpt-oss-20b`; secrets are read only by the API process.
- Official [Groq Structured Outputs](https://console.groq.com/docs/structured-outputs) lists `openai/gpt-oss-20b` for strict mode. The current milestone and scope JSON Schemas use the strict-required shape: each property is required and each object has `additionalProperties:false`; nullable values use null unions. Groq's docs demonstrate numeric `minimum`/`maximum` but do not list `minItems`/`maxItems`; the Groq request projection strips only those two undocumented array-length keywords. Existing shared Zod validation, evidence citation checks, fixed prompts, 25-second timeout, rate limits, and human approval remain in place.
- HTTP 429 and Groq capacity/server-unavailable responses map to safe retryable API errors; no automatic provider retries are performed. API keys and provider error bodies are not returned to callers or logged. No real Groq/OpenAI request has been made.
- Supabase remains untouched. A source review found that the currently applied `record_scope_analysis` RPC rejects every `p_provider` except `openai`. The API now records the selected provider truthfully, so Groq scope analysis cannot persist until the compatibility migration described below is approved and applied. Milestone generation can run through Groq without a database write; scope-analysis generation needs the migration before it can complete persistence.
- Setup instructions and `.env.example` now list `AI_PROVIDER=groq`, `GROQ_API_KEY`, and `GROQ_MODEL=openai/gpt-oss-20b`, plus the OpenAI alternative. The mocked regressions, TypeScript check, complete suite, production build, and secret-boundary/diff validation are recorded in the verification table above.

The migration changes no grants, policies, RLS state, tables, or data; PostgreSQL `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` retains the existing owner and ACL. The original 13 migration files were not edited.

#### Reliable staging invoice-preflight execution preparation (2026-11-02)

- Root cause confirmed: the Codex `functions.exec` wrapper awaited native `exec_command` but emitted only `.output`; when the command exceeded its 30-second initial wait, it returned a `session_id` and no final `exit_code`, both of which the wrapper discarded. The native process continued, but later output could not be retrieved. The corrected invocation pattern preserves stdout, exit code, and session ID, stores/polls a live session, and the runner now also writes recoverable checkpoints.
- Added `scripts/paypal-preflight-report.mjs`: a strict allowlist journal under ignored `.proofpay-local/`, append-only with `fsync` per checkpoint. It records stage/status, bounded HTTP/error codes, fixed check names/booleans, run/approval IDs, and final process exit code. Arbitrary fields—including credentials, tokens, headers, and raw provider/API responses—are dropped. Truncated final records do not hide earlier checkpoints.
- Updated the read-only preflight to run independent authenticated RLS reads and profile/invoice API reads, record each read and domain assertion separately, and continue other independent checks when one fails. A failed invoice/claim read cannot be mistaken for proof that no invoice/claim exists. The service-role client remains limited to the boolean existence check for the one create claim.
- Hardened the recovery runner: password reset is unavailable unless a valid explicit `--reset-approval-id=<UUID>` is supplied. It verifies the staging reference and exact Auth fixture before atomically creating an exclusive, fsynced approval marker and writing the durable reset-intent checkpoint. The owner-password login runner has no reset path and clears the local password variable after sign-in. PowerShell launchers emit report location and process exit status without writing credentials.

The current runners still sign out and exit; a no-owner-credential flow that holds a session in memory while awaiting separate invoice approval is intentionally not enabled in this read-only preparation. - The local one-time marker was then created and the authenticated `POST /api/invoices/{invoiceId}/send` route was invoked exactly once. It returned HTTP 409. No retry was made. The existing create claim is still `completed`, and its invoice number matches the invoice row.
- Root cause: `begin_paypal_invoice_send` inserts a send operation claim using the same invoice number as the existing create claim. The applied schema has a global unique index on `paypal_operation_claims(invoice_number)`, so the send-claim insert conflicts before the API reaches `PaypalClient.sendInvoice`. The API maps PostgreSQL unique-violation SQLSTATE `23505` to HTTP 409. The durable runner intentionally did not log the raw RPC error body/code; the collision is identified from the applied migration/function definitions, the matching completed create claim, the absent send claim, the 409 response, and the provider remaining `DRAFT`.
- The API send route was attempted once, but no PayPal send request was made and no successful send/email is confirmed. The marker now records the single route attempt and must not be cleared to retry. No payment, webhook, invoice creation, committed Supabase change, production operation, migration, or deployment occurred; the failed send-claim transaction rolled back.
- Next work requires a separately approved corrective migration preparation and review. The narrow candidate is to retain unique invoice numbers for `operation='create'` claims with a partial unique index, allowing the send claim to reuse its invoice number while the existing unique active-operation key continues to prevent duplicate sends. Do not apply it without separate staging approval. Any later Sandbox send is a new attempt and requires fresh authorization; payment and webhook tests remain separately unapproved.

#### PayPal Sandbox webhook readiness review (2026-11-02)

- Read-only source/configuration review only. No PayPal, Groq, or Supabase requests were made; no webhook was registered or edited; no invoice/payment operation, data/schema change, deployment, or production action occurred. The two consumed invoice-send authorization markers were left untouched.
- The backend exposes `POST /api/webhooks/paypal`. Its handler requires the PayPal transmission headers, uses the configured webhook ID, and calls PayPal's Sandbox `verify-webhook-signature` endpoint. A failed/indeterminate verification returns before the webhook database RPC. For allowlisted events, the server fetches the current invoice through the Invoicing API and passes that provider response—not the event's status or client-supplied data—to the service RPC.
- Presence-only `.env` check: Sandbox client ID and secret are configured; `PAYPAL_WEBHOOK_ID` is present but matches the template-placeholder rule and is not accepted by the application's configuration check. No credential values were printed. Therefore the API's webhook readiness flag is false and signature verification cannot currently succeed.
- No public callback URL is configured in the local environment: `APP_ORIGIN` and `VITE_API_URL` classify as non-HTTPS local URLs, and `RENDER_EXTERNAL_URL` is unset. `render.yaml` describes a possible API service but does not establish that it is deployed. Public HTTPS reachability is therefore unverified and unavailable from the inspected configuration; no network probe was made. The intended path is `/api/webhooks/paypal` on a publicly reachable HTTPS staging API.
- The route's event allowlist matches the seven Invoicing event types documented by PayPal: `INVOICING.INVOICE.CREATED`, `.SENT`, `.PAID`, `.CANCELLED`, `.REFUNDED`, `.UPDATED`, and `.SCHEDULED`. Other event types are signature-verified, acknowledged as unprocessed, and do not reach the database RPC. PayPal's current Invoicing docs list these event categories and identify the event ID as the deduplication key.
- Reliability/security follow-up from the official documentation review: PayPal's Invoicing webhook guide calls for a 2xx response within 30 seconds and describes a five-minute transmission-time check for direct signature verification. The current route synchronously performs postback verification, an invoice GET, and a database RPC; PayPal REST requests each have a 15-second timeout, so the cold path can exceed 30 seconds before database time. The code does not have a separate local transmission-time freshness check. Confirm the postback verifier's freshness semantics and resolve the response-time bound before relying on live delivery; preserve verified-event persistence, deduplication, and legitimate provider retries.
- The migration defines a unique `provider_event_id`; `apply_verified_paypal_webhook` inserts with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, returning a duplicate result without reapplying invoice state. The function is `SECURITY DEFINER`, verifies `auth.role() = 'service_role'`, and its migration revokes EXECUTE from `PUBLIC`, `anon`, and `authenticated`, granting it to `service_role`. The webhook events table has RLS enabled. The invoice update uses a row lock and only applies observations no older than the stored provider observation time.
- The local payment-state path maps provider statuses, sets `paid_at` only for a provider status mapped to `paid`, and does not accept a client-provided payment status. The webhook handler first verifies the signature and then obtains the current invoice state from PayPal. The authenticated invoice-reconciliation route also reads PayPal before changing local state. The service-role-only database RPC is the sole webhook state-write boundary identified in this review.
- Fixed-price milestone invoices and hourly-batch invoices share the `invoices` provider-ID reconciliation path; the webhook RPC is not source-type-specific. The code therefore supports both invoice origins at the state-update layer, but no live webhook or paid hourly invoice workflow has been verified. Current recorded real Sandbox activity covers a sent fixed-price milestone invoice; no payment was made.
- Existing API tests cover failed signature verification (no database RPC) and successful verification followed by authoritative PayPal invoice lookup and persistence. They do not exercise live PayPal delivery, duplicate-event persistence against PostgreSQL, delayed/out-of-order delivery, or an hourly-batch paid invoice. The local environment-import diagnostic using `tsx` hit a Node `uv_os_get_passwd` `ENOMEM`; the independent environment-file presence-only check succeeded without importing or printing secrets.

Vite continues to report the existing AG Grid chunk warning (719.63 kB minified). These are local checks, not live webhook validation.
- Readiness: webhook implementation is present, but Sandbox webhook readiness is **blocked** by the placeholder webhook ID and the absence of a configured/deployed public HTTPS callback. #### Hackathon demo Phase A — durable PayPal webhook processing prepared locally (2026-11-02)

- Replaced the webhook request's inline invoice lookup/update with signature verification followed by an atomic durable-inbox enqueue. The route preserves the original event JSON bytes for PayPal postback verification, validates the signed transmission timestamp format, allowlists the seven Invoicing event types, and returns HTTP 202 only after the enqueue RPC confirms an inserted or duplicate event. Queue errors, malformed receipts, and bounded timeouts return non-2xx so PayPal can redeliver. Unrelated verified event types receive HTTP 200 with `accepted: false` and are not stored for invoice processing.
- Added `PaypalWebhookWorker`: it claims verified jobs using database leases, processes up to 25 per pass, fetches the current invoice from PayPal, and completes through one transactional RPC. Provider event status is never used as invoice truth. A 45-second lease, row locking/`SKIP LOCKED`, a random fencing token, and expiry-based reclaim protect restart/concurrent-worker recovery. Retryable provider/database failures use bounded backoff; permanent failures, exhausted attempts, and expired max-attempt leases remain dead letters for explicit service-role operator requeue. The worker is started only after the API is listening and stops on server close.
- Prepared the unapplied migration `202611020004_paypal_webhook_durable_processing.sql` after the 17 existing applied versions. It adds only webhook queue metadata/indexing, service-role-only enqueue/claim/complete/retry/requeue RPCs, an exact provider status mapper, and a trigger preventing an already `paid` PayPal invoice from regressing to a nonterminal status while allowing refund states. Completion locks and updates only the invoice matched by PayPal provider invoice ID plus its event row; it does not modify milestone approvals or agreements. The same invoice lookup supports milestone and hourly-batch rows. No data/schema operation was run against Supabase; do not apply migration 18 without separate approval.
- Updated `paypal-plan.md`, API docs, and `docs/OPERATIONS.md` with the revised contract, bounded request budgets, host setup, Sandbox webhook registration steps, recovery flow, and current unverified external conditions. The demo checklist now distinguishes local implementation from unapplied migration, absent webhook ID/public HTTPS callback, unverified live signature freshness, and payment testing still requiring separate approval. PayPal documents HTTPS/443 callbacks and retry delivery; this code does not add a local five-minute cutoff, instead delegates signature verification to the PayPal postback endpoint and deduplicates by event ID. Confirm live postback freshness semantics during the later authorized Sandbox setup.
- Regression tests cover valid and failed signatures, exact signed event JSON preservation, allowlist filtering, duplicate delivery, queue failure/timeouts, bounded worker RPCs, transient retry and permanent dead-letter paths, provider timeouts/404s, completion failure and restart recovery, concurrent local passes, authoritative provider status over stale event content, paid-state protection, service-role-only RPC/table access, and the shared milestone/hourly invoice lookup.

#### Hackathon demo Phase A — migration 18 applied and verified on Staging (2026-11-02)

- Immediately before execution, the official Supabase CLI confirmed linked project `ProofPayAI Staging` / `staging-project-reference-redacted`; migrations 1–17 were applied and `202611020004_paypal_webhook_durable_processing.sql` was the only pending migration. `supabase db push --linked --dry-run --skip-vault --yes` previewed exactly migration 18. The reviewed migration SHA-256 was `867030A19A950B0F9DCDA122837FD8C5511A6F4CA53DF421A910428F3A2AD8AE`.
- Applied only migration 18 once with the official Supabase CLI (`supabase db push --linked --skip-vault --yes`); it exited successfully. The subsequent linked migration list shows all 18 local/remote versions match, and another official dry-run reports the database is up to date with no pending migrations.
- Read-only PostgreSQL catalog checks verified all seven queue columns (`verified_at`, `attempt_count`, `next_attempt_at`, `last_attempt_at`, `lease_token`, `lease_expires_at`, `dead_lettered_at`), the attempt-count check constrained to 0–8, the unique provider-event ID index, the partial retry index, and absence of the retired unprocessed index. The queue table has RLS enabled. `anon` and `authenticated` have no SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE/TRIGGER table privilege and cannot CREATE in `public`; the table has no user policies. The intended worker role retains SELECT/UPDATE access.
- Catalog/function-definition checks verified that enqueue, claim, completion, retry, and dead-letter requeue are `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs with fixed `search_path`, executable only by `service_role`, and each worker mutation checks `auth.role()`. Enqueue records `verified_at`; claiming uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, a 45-second lease, and an eight-attempt limit; retry backoff is capped at one hour; completion fences by lease token and orders invoice updates by provider observation time. The old synchronous webhook state-write RPC is no longer executable even by `service_role`. The paid-state regression trigger is enabled. No queue events currently exist, so live lease/retry/dead-letter transitions remain unexercised.
- The migration performs no DML against invoices, PayPal operation claims, milestones, or agreements. Pre/post Staging counts remain one invoice and two operation claims; the claims are one completed `create` and one completed `send`. Read-only verification confirms the existing local PayPal invoice remains linked to the approved milestone, USD 60,000 minor units, status `sent`; the milestone remains `approved`. No invoice, claim marker, payment, webhook event, milestone, or agreement was created or changed by this operation.
- After the database change, the focused SQL-contract check `node --test scripts/validate-paypal-webhook-durable-processing.test.mjs` passed 7/7; focused PayPal API and worker tests passed 38/38; `npm.cmd run typecheck` passed for shared/API/web; `npm.cmd test` passed all 275 tests (24 shared, 117 API, 15 web, 119 script/SQL-contract); and `npm.cmd run build` passed. The production build still reports the known AG Grid chunk warning at 719.63 kB minified / 198.47 kB gzip. PostgreSQL verification was performed against the real non-production Staging database; local PostgreSQL/Docker remains unavailable for a second engine run.
- No Render or other deployment, PayPal webhook registration/update, provider webhook call, payment/simulation, invoice operation, Groq request, production action, PowerShell policy change, or send-marker operation occurred. - Render's current Free-service documentation says a Free web service sleeps after 15 minutes without inbound HTTP/WebSocket traffic and takes about a minute to spin back up; Free services share 750 instance-hours per workspace/month. The static site has no compute charge. Workspace bandwidth/build limits still apply, and depending on payment settings, overages can suspend services or be billed. The worker shares the API process, so it stops during sleep. A cold webhook may therefore be delayed, and Free hosting cannot guarantee continuously available webhook processing or timely automatic reconciliation.
- The existing worker polls Supabase every second while the API process is awake, up to 86,400 idle claim RPCs/day if continuously active. Outbound database polling does not prevent the inbound-traffic sleep timer; Render documents potential suspension for uncommonly high service-initiated Internet traffic, including external database access. This preparation did not change the polling code. A separately approved follow-up could add immediate in-process wake after a verified enqueue and bounded exponential idle backoff, or keep this same durable queue/worker on an always-on API plan. No pinger or artificial keep-alive is proposed.
- The operations runbook now gives a truthful demo warm-up plan using normal presenter sign-in/profile/project requests, then `/health`, `/api/status`, and authenticated reads; it warns that the first API request may cold-start and that status booleans do not prove provider reachability. Previously verified Groq requests were run locally, not on Render. The existing fictional Sandbox invoice remains SENT and is not to be recreated/resent/modified. No payment or webhook reconciliation is verified; the demo must not claim a paid state. No provider calls were made in this preparation.

#### Frontend Linux native binding lockfile correction (2026-11-04)

- Verified the Render static-site deploy `dep-dbl550qjnfac73ek7trg` at commit `8d8b82e2d3b344c2247f5ac9c6a2259f8251bafe` failed while loading Vite configuration: Tailwind Oxide raised `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` for `@tailwindcss/oxide-linux-x64-gnu` and `tailwindcss-oxide.linux-x64-gnu.node`. Render reported the deploy was triggered by Blueprint sync. The error occurred after `npm ci`, before Vite could build the frontend.
- The root lock entry for `@tailwindcss/oxide` 4.3.3 already declared the platform packages in `optionalDependencies`, but the lockfile contained only the Windows x64 MSVC package record. Its installed Windows counterpart was present locally; the Linux x64 GNU package record was absent. This establishes a Linux-incomplete lockfile and is consistent with Windows-platform lock generation; the lockfile itself does not prove which exact command originally generated it.
- Added only `apps/web/node_modules/@tailwindcss/oxide-linux-x64-gnu` to `package-lock.json`, locked at 4.3.3 with the official npm tarball and SHA-512 integrity, Linux/x64 platform selectors, optional status, MIT license metadata, and Node >=20 engine. `package.json` files and every existing dependency version are unchanged. A clean Windows `NODE_ENV=production npm ci --include=dev` succeeded; npm added the existing locked dependency set. The Linux binary is correctly not installed on this Windows host.
- Read-only npm security audit of the corrected lockfile reported 0 vulnerabilities (0 info, low, moderate, high, or critical) across 322 dependencies. The registry metadata verified version 4.3.3, `os: linux`, and `cpu: x64` for the new lock entry.

#### Freelancer profile save validation correction (2026-11-04)

- Root cause confirmed against `freelancerProfileInputSchema` in `packages/shared/src/profile.ts`: the strict request schema accepts `hourlyRateMinor` and rejects unknown keys, while the profile form draft also has a UI-only `hourlyRate` string. Spreading the draft into `POST /api/profile/freelancer` sent that unsupported key and caused the API validation failure.
- Added an explicit typed serializer in `apps/web/src/pages/ProfilePage.tsx`. It sends the supported profile fields only, converts the hourly preference through the existing exact minor-unit conversion into `hourlyRateMinor`, and does not serialize `hourlyRate`. The backend strict schema and validation rules are unchanged.
- Added `apps/web/src/pages/ProfilePage.test.tsx`: one regression verifies the serialized request passes the shared strict Zod schema and converts USD 125.50 to 12,550 minor units; a mocked UI test saves a private freelancer profile and verifies its headline and hourly preference reload from the mocked API after remount. #### Milestone evidence and revision-feedback visibility correction (2026-11-05)

- Root cause in `GET /api/projects/:projectId`: the authenticated project-detail projection returned milestone workflow status but never selected `public.deliverables` or `public.milestone_reviews`. The existing submission and review routes call the authenticated `submit_milestone_deliverable` and `review_milestone` RPCs; the source RPCs persist summary/evidence and review decision/note. The historical fixture ledger records an earlier approved milestone, but this does not establish the current state from the user's newer two-account test.
- The API now first resolves the project through the request-scoped authenticated Supabase client and returns 404 before querying child evidence if RLS hides the project. For a visible project, both new table reads explicitly filter by `project_id`; the existing `authenticated` SELECT grants and `is_project_member(project_id)` RLS policies remain unchanged. The deliverable query does not select `storage_object_path`; its existing one-of URL/path constraint lets the response derive only a private-file-present boolean from a null URL. Response projections also omit reviewer UUIDs and idempotency keys.
- The project detail now shows submission summaries, chronological evidence history, review decisions, and revision feedback to either participant after the normal authenticated detail reload. Evidence links are made clickable only when they parse as HTTPS URLs without embedded credentials. React renders summaries and notes as text. Private file storage paths and public storage URLs are not exposed; the UI states that secure file preview/download is not configured.
- Read-only Staging preflight: the linked reference and official project listing matched ProofPayAI Staging. One read-only SQL summary query failed with a sanitized transport error, so fresh persistence of the user's specific submission/revision note and current milestone state could not be verified. No password/session retrieval, database write, record modification, migration, or provider call occurred. The project-member RLS policy was verified from the existing migration source, not from a fresh authenticated cross-account database session.
- Regression coverage: API tests verify both record collections are returned for a visible authenticated project, filtered by the requested project, private paths/idempotency fields are omitted, and an unrelated user receives 404 before child evidence queries. UI tests mount the persisted project response as both client and freelancer, verify summaries/HTTPS evidence/revision notes and private-file messaging, and reject unsafe evidence schemes while rendering user text without HTML interpretation.

#### Fixed-price project completion workflow — local implementation (2026-11-05)

- Added local, unapplied migration `202611050001_project_completion_workflow.sql` after the 18-migration Staging baseline last recorded above. It does not backfill or rewrite project rows. Completion is evaluated only after an approved milestone review or an authenticated participant recheck. The guarded decision requires an active fixed-price project, the current accepted agreement, acceptance records for both project roles tied to current project members, at least one milestone for that agreement, every such milestone approved, and matching project/agreement/milestone totals and currency.
- Open scope requests, proposals, and draft or awaiting amendments block completion. An analyzed request is treated as resolved only when a rejected change-order version exists; withdrawn/rejected work does not itself block. Every accepted change order blocks completion because the schema has no link proving its added work is represented by current completion criteria. Hourly projects are excluded. Completed status is separate from invoice/payment state; the migration only changes project status and appends one `project.completed` activity on the successful status transition.
- Workflow RPCs take a project-scoped transaction advisory lock and then lock the project row before their existing child-row operations. The existing milestone review RPC remains the authority for client role, review state, and idempotency; its wrapper invokes the completion guard after approval. A project trigger and guards on agreement, milestone, participant, and scope operations reject implicit reopening or adding work after completion. A future reopening workflow needs separate design and must preserve the historical completion event.
- Added authenticated `POST /api/projects/:projectId/recheck-completion`, backed by a participant-checked RPC using the same completion guard. The project-detail page offers the action for eligible fixed-price projects with an accepted current agreement and all its milestones approved; results refresh from the persisted project. Project list/detail status labels now show `Completed`. No invoice creation, invoice state, payment state, milestone review history, agreement version, evidence, or existing project record is changed by the recheck implementation itself.
- For the previously user-verified fictional Staging project, after this migration is separately approved and applied, either existing client or freelancer should sign in normally, open that project's detail page, and use **Recheck project completion** if the current agreement and milestones meet the visible preconditions. The RPC rechecks membership, agreement acceptances, totals, scope requests and change orders under the project lock. It may safely leave the project `in_progress` with a blocker reason; it will not guess about accepted added work or alter financial state. No fresh Staging read or recheck was performed in this local phase.
- Added API authorization/RPC tests, project-detail recheck and completed-state tests, a project-list Completed-label test, and SQL contract checks for completion guards, lock/event behavior, scope/amendment policy, fixed-only handling, historical recheck, grants, and payment-state separation. Existing scope-migration ordering checks now place this new migration after webhook migration 18; the prior 18 migration files remain unchanged.
- Verification on 2026-11-05: `npm.cmd test` passed 244 tests (24 shared, 124 API, 22 web, 74 SQL/Blueprint contract tests); `npm.cmd run typecheck` passed; `npm.cmd run build` passed. The production build still reports the existing AG Grid chunk-size warning (719.63 kB minified / 198.47 kB gzip). `git diff --check` passed; Git emitted only its existing LF-to-CRLF normalization notices.
- No local PostgreSQL server or Docker runtime is available, so the migration was checked with static SQL contract tests but was not executed against PostgreSQL. Concurrency and transactional behavior therefore still need validation on an isolated PostgreSQL database before migration application. Staging migration history and the fictional project's current records were not rechecked or modified. No Supabase, PayPal, Groq, Render, Git commit, or Git push operation occurred.

The member-facing policies are SELECT-only and project-member scoped. `anon` has no direct SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE privileges on the inspected tables; `authenticated` has SELECT only on member-data tables and no direct INSERT/UPDATE; invoice claim and webhook queue tables are not selectable by `authenticated`. The backend `service_role` has privileged table access as expected and must remain server-only.
- Existing auth workflow functions are present, `SECURITY DEFINER`, and use fixed `pg_catalog, public, extensions` search paths. `authenticated` can execute them; `anon` cannot. Their ACLs also include `service_role`, matching current Supabase public-schema default function ACLs; the relevant functions enforce `auth.uid()` and participant-role checks, and the API uses request-scoped user clients for normal participant operations. `service_role` is not a member of `authenticated`. Migration 19 explicitly revokes `service_role` from its participant-facing wrappers/unlocked originals. No anon/public execution hole was observed. This default-ACL behavior is recorded as a least-privilege consideration, not identified as a current user-facing bypass.
- Migration 19 dependencies have no catalog conflict: required columns and baseline functions are present, and its new trigger/function names are unused. Its SQL is transaction-wrapped and does not rewrite existing project, milestone, invoice, or agreement rows. Future exact procedure (after separate apply authorization): verify linked ref/history again; run `supabase db push --linked --skip-vault --dry-run` and stop unless it previews only `202611050001_project_completion_workflow.sql`; after approval, apply once with `supabase db push --linked --skip-vault`; then query remote history and read-only catalog for migration 19, trigger/function definitions, grants, RLS, and unchanged record counts/statuses. `--skip-vault` avoids applying unrelated Vault config. On any error/uncertainty, do not retry or repair history; inspect actual history/schema and use only an explicitly approved Supabase recovery process. Migration 19 is ready for separate deployment approval, subject to its final immediate preflight.
- Scope workflow design only; no new business semantics were coded. Recommended rules for approval: (1) the requesting client may withdraw its own `pending_analysis` or `analyzed` request only before any change-order version exists; store an append-only actor/time/reason decision and activity event. (2) An analyzed request that is not withdrawn should close as “no amendment” only after both client and freelancer explicitly confirm; requests with draft/awaiting/accepted amendment versions cannot use the close path and must proceed through amendment resolution. Rejection remains an explicit decision with existing history retained. (3) An accepted change order must not itself silently change the agreement or count as completed work. Require a new immutable agreement version that incorporates the accepted amendment and a two-party-approved milestone schedule representing the resulting full contractual obligations. Preserve old agreement, milestone, evidence, review, and acceptance history; mark the new version current only after both parties accept. Completion should evaluate exactly one current obligation schedule, reconcile exact minor-unit totals, require every current obligation approved, and block pending decisions or unrepresented amendments. All request closure, amendment incorporation, agreement activation, and completion checks must use the project workflow lock before child-row locks; no invoice/payment state participates.
- Schema/design choices needing approval before implementation: whether request withdrawal is requester-only or requires counterparty acknowledgment; whether “no amendment” closure requires both parties (recommended); and the versioned milestone-schedule representation/activation protocol for accepted amendments. These rules determine a new SQL migration and cannot be safely inferred from the current schema.
- No self-contained application change was unambiguous without those rules; no app source or migration was modified. No temporary catalog-query file remains. The four pre-existing modified paths were preserved; only this canonical tracker was appended.
- Final verification run — `git diff --check` exited 0 after the preflight/design entry. Git reported only existing LF-to-CRLF working-copy notices. Final status: branch and HEAD unchanged; the only modified paths are the tracker plus the three pre-existing scope-test/migration-README changes; the temporary SQL query file is absent.

Local `.dev.vars` files are ignored; the committed example has placeholders only.

#### Independent Cloudflare webhook review corrections (2026-11-07)

- Corrected PayPal invoice snapshot ordering after the independent review found that completion time was not a provider version. `PaypalClient` now parses `detail.metadata.last_update_time` as the canonical provider update time and accepts the invoice-object sample's `detail.metadata.update_time` only as a fallback. Missing values become `null`; a present malformed timestamp rejects the provider snapshot; the worker sends provider version and local `provider_observed_at` separately. The APIMatic/PayPal contract sheet records this before the code change.
- Prepared local-only migration 20, `202611070001_paypal_provider_snapshot_ordering.sql`. It adds `invoices.provider_updated_at`, orders snapshots by provider time (with observation-time fallback only while no provider version is stored), guards payment/refund/cancellation status transitions in both the completion RPC and invoice trigger, and keeps webhook lease, event-ID, invoice-ID, service-role, and deduplication checks. Migration 19 remains in position 19 and the new migration is position 20; the scope migration-order validators now assert both facts. Migration 20 is not applied to any database.
- Added a deterministic two-worker race test where an older PAID provider response returns after a newer REFUNDED response; the mocked durable completion receipt preserves the refund and logs that the stale snapshot was not applied. Added API analytics assertions confirming refunded and partially-refunded invoices do not contribute to the existing exact-minor-unit “currently paid” total. The pre-existing app rule remains unchanged: only `status = paid` contributes; no refund amount is inferred.
- Reduced the Cloudflare Queue database-drain cap from 25 to 8 rows per invocation; Cron remains capped at one row and Queue continuation remains a wake hint. Estimated Free-plan external subrequests, assuming one fetch per Supabase RPC and no redirects/retries: ingress up to 3 (cold OAuth, verification, enqueue); Queue up to 26 for eight normally completed rows and 34 if each completion also needs a retry RPC; repeated OAuth failure up to 25; Cron approximately 5 normally or 6 on completion failure. Thus the reviewed Queue path retains at least 16 requests of headroom below the documented 50 external-subrequest limit. Queue `max_batch_size: 1` still limits wake messages, not rows drained.
- Corrected the test-TypeScript harness issues and added the repeatable `typecheck:webhook-tests` command. Preserved all prior tests; the two existing migration-order validators initially failed because they assumed migration 19 stayed last, then passed after being updated to require the payment-ordering migration 20 to follow it.
